## Cycle 19 — 2026-09-08

### CEO Summary — Gap 10 (RSD stickers) scored 30/40 and briefed; Gap Finder corrected two of Cycle 18's product-type claims (RSD wall art and Mob wife wall art are both already served, not white space); a second, weaker candidate gap logged as low-priority, 2026-09-08

**Run log:** completed 2026-09-08 · next cycle scheduled 2026-09-09 · Trend Scout: skipped (Cycle 18's scouting pass is only one day old; today's actionable item was scoring/briefing its output, not fresh scouting) · Phrase Miner: success (weak, single-example formulas extracted for 6 of Cycle 18's rising/thin candidates) · Gap Finder: success (2 gaps scored — Gap 10 at 30/40, Gap 11 at 18/40 — plus 5 candidates ruled out as already-served) · Review Miner: success (insufficient data — no `ETSY_API_KEY`, 19th consecutive cycle unset) · Design Briefer: success (Brief 10 drafted from phrase/style formulas alone, Review Miner had nothing to contribute)

**Scope decision: partial recheck, four sub-agents.** No CEO Summary existed yet for today. The two standing blockers (`ETSY_API_KEY` unset, Gap 6's retire/keep call) are unchanged since Cycle 2/3, so re-touching either would only restate facts already on record. Cycle 18's own "Open follow-ups" named a specific, concrete, unblocked next step: run Phrase Miner + Gap Finder on the six rising/thin candidates it had just logged (AuDHD, HSP, Misophonia, RSD, Mob wife aesthetic, Trad wife aesthetic), prioritizing RSD as the flagged standout. I sent Phrase Miner first (it needs Trend Scout's raw findings, which I supplied from Cycle 18's own tracker rows since that scouting pass is only a day old — no need to re-scout), then Gap Finder with Phrase Miner's formulas plus the existing tracked-niche listing counts for cross-referencing. Since Gap Finder's top result (RSD stickers) scored comparably to the pipeline's other briefed gaps (30/40, tied with Gap 8), I continued the pipeline order through Review Miner (expected insufficient data, confirmed) and Design Briefer to produce Brief 10 this same cycle, rather than deferring briefing to a future cycle the way Cycle 18 deferred scoring.

**Phrase Miner** extracted weak, single-example title/copy formulas for all six candidates — every niche in this batch had exactly one example title surfaced by Trend Scout, so nothing cleared "strong pattern." It correctly declined to invent visual-style formulas where the title gave no textual basis (AuDHD, Mob wife aesthetic), and flagged real but single-source visual cues where they existed (HSP: neutral "white and beige" palette; Misophonia: "boho rainbow"; Trad wife: explicit "mid-century modern kitchen"; RSD: an inferred mascot/character-illustration approach implied by its one comp's pun).

**Gap Finder's findings, reviewed — including two corrections to Cycle 18's own framing that I'm incorporating into the Niches table below, not silently:**
- **RSD wall art is not a void — it's already served.** Cycle 18 logged RSD as "stickers (ADHD-adjacent; could extend to wall art)." Gap Finder found 3 real, resolvable poster/wall-art listings already combining RSD with ADHD framing. The RSD *sticker* gap (Gap 10 below) is real and product-type-specific, not a niche-wide opportunity — I've corrected RSD's Niches-table row to reflect this rather than let the earlier "could extend to wall art" framing stand uncorrected.
- **Mob wife wall art is thinner-served than Cycle 18's search pass suggested, but not empty.** Cycle 18 said "wall-art market page exists but no matching individual wall-art listing surfaced this pass." Gap Finder found 2 real wall-art listings plus a dedicated `mob_wife_aesthetic_decor` market page. I've corrected Mob wife's Niches-table row accordingly. This is new, more thorough search finding a prior pass missed — not a retirement of the niche itself, which stays tracked and unresolved either way.
- **Gap 10 — RSD stickers** (bridging tracked ADHD/neurodivergent stickers [3,962] × chronic illness/spoonie stickers [3,204]): scored **30/40** (Market Opportunity 9, Ease of Entry 7, User Fit 8, Profit Potential 6). Near-zero (1) confirmed physical-sticker supply between two large, validated parent niches — the strongest opportunity signal Gap Finder found this cycle. Real risk: mental-health-topic tone handling, and genuine uncertainty on true demand size since no listing-count data exists for "RSD" as a standalone term (no API key). This is the cycle's headline finding — see Brief 10 below.
- **Gap 11 (stretch, low confidence) — Trad wife × Nonnacore domestic-homemaker wall art**: scored **18/40** (Market Opportunity 3, Ease of Entry 5, User Fit 6, Profit Potential 4) — Gap Finder's own words: "do not prioritize this cycle." This is *inferred*, not directly search-confirmed either way (search found separate `nonna` and `tradwife` market pages, no combined listing, but couldn't rule the intersection in or out with confidence). It rests on the weaker of the two source niches (trad wife itself remains unvalidated — Trend Scout found effectively one listing, no dedicated market page) and carries real audience-merge uncertainty (trad wife skews young/pinup-glam, nonnacore skews elderly/cottage — they may not share a buyer) plus a genuine tone/political-sensitivity risk distinct from mechanical listing difficulty (trad wife is a contested identity term). Per pipeline rule 4, I'm logging it rather than discarding it — it's now the tracker's second-weakest scored gap, below even Gap 6 (20/40) — but I'm not briefing it this cycle; it needs trad wife itself independently validated with real data first, which is now added to the follow-ups list below.
- **Ruled out (already-served, not gaps):** AuDHD × ADHD/neurodivergent stickers (heavily served — multiple market pages and 5-6 distinct listings found, confirming this reads as a keyword sub-slice of the already-saturated parent niche, not a distinct gap); Misophonia × ADHD/spoonie/chronic-illness stickers (already actively claimed — one listing explicitly stacks all four terms); HSP × Empath positioning (not a genuine gap — sellers already fully conflate the two terms in the same listings); Mob wife × Nonnacore/Italian-grandma-kitchen aesthetic (already served, per the correction above). Two pairings (Misophonia × Afrohemian decor, HSP × Neo Deco wall art) were considered pre-search and correctly not pursued — no real product-type or audience-overlap signal to justify spending a search on them.

**Review Miner** checked for `ETSY_API_KEY` (still unset, 19th consecutive cycle) and correctly stopped rather than improvising buyer language for the RSD-stickers niche — reported `insufficient data — no API key` and nothing else, per its no-fallback rule.

**Design Briefer drafted Brief 10 (RSD Stickers)** from Phrase Miner's single-comp formula and Gap Finder's rubric scores alone, since Review Miner had nothing to contribute this cycle. It explicitly avoided reusing the one real competitor's specific pun (that's their own joke, not a reusable formula element), instead generalizing the formula shape (validation-forward title, mascot/character illustration, clinical-term + community-slang keyword stack) into original copy. Confidence labeled **thin** — one real comp, no buyer-language grounding, no comp pricing — with Tier 1 (~$60–85) recommended and Tier 2 explicitly framed as signal-gathering (not a scale-up commitment) given Gap Finder's own moderate Profit Potential score (6/10) and the flagged sensitive-topic tone risk. Full brief is below.

**Nothing retired.** All 18 originally-tracked niches, all 13 of Cycle 18's new niches, Gaps 1–9, and Briefs 1–5/7/8/9 (Brief 6 still deliberately unbriefed) carry forward unchanged except the two corrected Niches-table notes (RSD, Mob wife) described above — those are corrections from new, more thorough search evidence, not retirements or score changes to anything previously scored. ### Gap Scores — niche-validation rubric (restated in full; rows 10–11 are new, rows 1–9 unchanged from Cycles 1/7/8/14)

1-10 per category, scored from real data gathered this cycle (Etsy listing counts, saturation levels, Review Miner findings where available) — never inferred from vibes alone. User Fit is scored specifically for this operator: CS student, technical/automation-minded, limited budget and time. Total = sum out of 40. Full scoring guidance lives in `.claude/agents/gap-finder.md`.

| Rank | Gap | Market Opportunity | Ease of Entry | User Fit | Profit Potential | Total | Rationale |

**What this makes explicit:** Gap 10 ties Gap 8 as the second-highest-scoring tracked gap (30/40), behind only Gap 4 (32/40) — a genuine, high-confidence Market Opportunity read (9/10, the highest of any gap) tempered by real tone-risk and audience-size uncertainty in the other three categories. Gap 11 is now the tracker's lowest-scoring entry (18/40), below even Gap 6's long-standing 20/40 — both remain tracked stretch entries, neither retired.

### Design Briefs (restated in full; Brief 10 is new, Briefs 1–5/7/8/9 unchanged from Cycles 1/8/9/11/14, Brief 6 still unbriefed, Gap 11 not briefed this cycle — see Gaps section)

Titles: primary keyword front-loaded in the first 3 words, lead with outcome not feature, "best/amazing/premium/quality" banned outright. Descriptions: hook → "who this is for" line → `[Feature] → [benefit]` bullets → specific CTA. See `.claude/agents/design-briefer.md` for the full rule set.

**BRIEF 1 — Knightcore × School Nurse/Teacher**
- **Exact title text:** "School Nurse Knight Tee, Healer of the Realm Medieval Shirt, Comfort Colors Ren Faire Nurse Gift, Fantasy Educator Appreciation"
- **Listing description:** Tired of nurse-week gifts that could've come from anyone? This one couldn't. Made for school nurses and clinical educators who'd rather wear a crest than a cartoon cupcake mug — the ones who read romantasy on lunch break and know exactly what "Healer of the Realm" means. Comfort Colors garment-dyed cotton → broken-in soft the day it arrives, not after ten washes. Hand-drawn medieval crest linework → reads as real art up close, not clipart across a classroom. Unisex fit → grab your size off the standard chart, no guessing. Order before nurse appreciation week to guarantee it's on her desk, not in transit.
- **On-product graphic text:** **"HEALER OF THE REALM"** with small subtext **"School Nurse Est. [Year]"**
- **Style direction:** Comfort Colors garment-dyed faded tee (sage or faded black); minimalist medieval line-art icon — a caduceus redrawn as a crossed sword-and-staff crest; goth-floral border (roses/thorns) around the crest; centered badge/crest layout, single ink color for the linework.
- **Product type:** T-shirt
- **Target audience:** School nurses and clinical educators who read fantasy romance/romantasy or are into cottagecore-adjacent medieval aesthetics — gift-buyable for nurse appreciation week/graduation.
- **Suggested price:** $26–$28 (comp-verified: real castlecore/knightcore Comfort Colors tees on Etsy run $26.05–$28; one outlier listing at $68.89, treat as non-representative).
- **Under $100 to test:** Commission a single crest illustration via Fiverr (~$25–40), upload to Printify (free), order one physical sample tee (~$15–20 incl. shipping), list on Etsy ($0.20 fee). Total: ~$45–65.
- **Under $1,000 to scale:** Commission 3–4 colorway/subtext variants of the crest (~$100–150), list across 2–3 blank colors (sage, faded black, faded navy), run a small Etsy Ads test (~$150–300 over 1–2 months), sample each variant (~$60–80). Total: ~$400–700.
- **Fully scalable:** Template the same crest-and-motto formula across other occupation×subculture combos (medic, vet-tech, dental-hygienist) — swap subtext programmatically via Printify's API, batch-upload via their bulk tooling, and feed real sales data back into this tracker next cycle to replace the assumed conversion rate with actuals.
- Confidence: partially grounded — style/phrase formulas solid, but Castlecore/Knightcore's review sample this cycle was too thin (11 reviews, need 15+) for real buyer-language grounding. **Review Miner: not used** (insufficient data) — description hook is written from general market positioning, not quoted buyers.

**BRIEF 2 — Nonnacore × ADHD/Spoonie Self-Care Mug**
- **Exact title text:** "Spoonie Self-Care Mug, Nonna Says Rest Now, Personalized Chronic Illness Gift, Cozy Permission To Pause"
- **Listing description:** Some mornings the only productive thing you do is convince yourself it's okay to sit back down. This mug says that out loud, gently, before you even ask. Made for spoonies who want their self-care merch to feel like a hug from Nonna, not another wellness lecture. Custom name on the rim → unmistakably yours, or exactly right as a gift for the spoonie in your life. Dishwasher-safe ceramic → survives real mornings, not just the photo shoot. Cozy majolica-floral border → doesn't announce "sick" to anyone who glances at your desk, it just looks like your favorite mug. Add the name that needs the reminder most and let Nonna do the talking.
- **On-product graphic text:** **"Nonna Said Sit Down And Rest"** with small script tagline **"[Custom Name]'s Spoonie Corner"**
- **Style direction:** Warm terracotta/olive palette from Nonnacore, retro Italian majolica floral border around the rim; soft rounded script typography for "Nonna Said..." (cozy permission-giving tone, not edgy/sarcastic); small holographic spoon icon tucked into the floral border as a nod to spoon theory.
- **Product type:** Mug
- **Target audience:** Women 25–45 managing chronic illness/ADHD who respond to "cozy grandmother energy" self-care messaging rather than clinical or sarcastic framing.
- **Suggested price:** $18–$22 (comp-verified: real spoonie mugs on Etsy cluster $14–$27, personalized/custom listings trend toward the upper half).
- **Under $100 to test:** Commission the floral-border + spoon-icon art (~$30–50), set up Printify's personalization app for the custom-name field (free), order one sample mug (~$15–20 incl. shipping), list on Etsy ($0.20). Total: ~$50–75.
- **Under $1,000 to scale:** A/B test two taglines against the review-grounded "cozy, not clinical" framing, add 2 more colorway/rim-border variants, run a small Etsy Ads test (~$150–250), sample 3–4 variants (~$60). Total: ~$300–500.
- **Fully scalable:** Script personalization-field validation to catch typos/formatting before production — directly addresses the wording-accuracy complaint pattern Review Miner surfaced elsewhere in this niche family — and extend the same "cozy identity + custom name" template to adjacent self-care products (candles, tote bags) via Printify's catalog API.
- Confidence: well-grounded for the ADHD/spoonie half (52 + 54 real reviews — strong pattern: relatable humor as coping, non-clinical framing preferred). **Review Miner: partially used** — Nonnacore itself had only 3 reviews sampled (insufficient), so the "cozy grandmother" framing still comes from Phrase Miner's formula, not quoted Nonnacore buyers.

**BRIEF 3 — Gothmas × Pet-Mom Christmas Sticker**
- **Exact title text:** "Gothic Dog Mom Sticker, Skull & Ornament Christmas Decal, Kiss-Cut Vinyl Holiday Gift For Goth Pet Owners"
- **Listing description:** Every "gothic Christmas" sticker on Etsy right now either isn't goth enough or takes six weeks to show up. This one's neither. For dog moms who decorate in black and green before they decorate in red and gold. Thick kiss-cut vinyl → survives your water bottle, your car window, and your dog's curiosity, not the thin, flimsy stuff other shops sell. Blackletter type + skull-and-holly design → reads as intentional gothic style, not a last-minute craft-store add-on. Shipped fast with real tracking updates → no "still processing" three weeks later. Grab it now — Christmas doesn't wait for slow shipping, and neither should your dog's ornament collar.
- **On-product graphic text:** **"Goth Mom, Merry Christmas"** styled as blackletter, wrapped around a dog silhouette wearing a small ornament collar.
- **Style direction:** Black, dark green, and blood-red palette; blackletter/gothic type for the headline; skull-and-ornament hybrid iconography (skull with holly instead of a Santa hat); die-cut kiss-cut vinyl sticker.
- **Product type:** Sticker (kiss-cut, waterproof vinyl)
- **Target audience:** Goth-identifying pet owners already buying from "goth mommy"/"gothic dog" Etsy sellers, wanting a seasonal add-on.
- **Suggested price:** $3.50–$5 (standard Etsy sticker convention — not comp-verified, flagged as estimate).
- **Under $100 to test:** Commission the skull-and-ornament design (~$25–40), order a small physical proof pack (~$10–15), list on Etsy ($0.20). Total: ~$35–55.
- **Under $1,000 to scale:** Commission 3–4 breed-specific variants (review data shows buyers care about their dog matching), add a holographic-finish upsell, run a small Etsy Ads test (~$150–250) timed for the Nov–Dec window, hold buffer stock for ship-time. Total: ~$300–500.
- **Fully scalable:** Template the "gothic + [holiday] + [pet-mom]" formula across other holidays (Halloween, Valentine's) via scripted seasonal-icon swaps on the same base vector art, and cross-list via Printify's multi-channel tools once the Etsy version validates.
- Confidence: well-grounded — both source niches (Gothmas 52 reviews, Pet breed+hobby 15) cleared the review threshold. **Review Miner: used** — the fast/communicative-shipping promise in the description is drawn directly from repeated real shipping complaints in both pooled samples (including two 1★ reviews), not invented positioning.

**BRIEF 4 — Sobriety × Pet-Mom Sticker/Shirt**
- **Exact title text:** "Sober Dog Mom Sticker, Subtle Recovery Decal For Sponsor Sponsee Gifts, Waterproof ODAAT Pet Owner Design"
- **Listing description:** Most recovery merch either shouts it from across the room or hides it so well it means nothing to the one person you're gifting it to. Made for sponsors and sponsees who want a reminder that reads clearly to the people who need to see it — and as "just a cute dog sticker" to everyone else. Waterproof, dishwasher-tested vinyl → stays put on a tumbler through months of real use, not just the first wash. Correct, checked wording → no misprints or out-of-order text to second-guess when you give it as a gift. Sized for water bottles, laptops, and car windows → one sticker, everywhere you actually are. Pick one up for a sponsee's next milestone, or for the dog mom who's earned both titles.
- **On-product graphic text:** **"Sober AND a Dog Mom (Barely Surviving Either)"** *(Review Miner flag: real buyers in this niche describe wanting **discreet** signaling — "not super obvious to people who don't know what it means" — this overt/playful graphic may be worth revisiting with a fresh Phrase Miner pass next cycle; not changed unilaterally here since no new phrase formula was reported this cycle.)*
- **Style direction:** Clean inspirational-quote minimalism (not clinical) crossed with a simple line-art dog icon; broken-chain motif reworked as a dog-leash link breaking open; soft neutral palette (sage, cream, warm gray) to keep pet-mom warmth in the mix.
- **Product type:** Sticker (primary); same design adaptable to a Comfort Colors tee as a secondary SKU.
- **Target audience:** People in recovery (1+ year sobriety milestone, sponsors buying for sponsees) who are also active pet owners — sold as a milestone/anniversary gift, not daily-wear identity statement.
- **Suggested price:** $4–$5 sticker pack (convention, not comp-verified); $24–$26 if produced as a tee (in line with Brief 1's Comfort Colors comps).
- **Under $100 to test:** Commission the leash-link + dog-icon design (~$25–40), order a sticker proof pack (~$10–15), list the sticker SKU on Etsy ($0.20) — defer the tee SKU to the next tier. Total: ~$35–55.
- **Under $1,000 to scale:** Add the Comfort Colors tee SKU (sample 2–3 tees, ~$60–90), proof-read wording carefully before production — directly addresses the real wording-accuracy complaint Review Miner found in this niche — run a small Etsy Ads test (~$150–250) targeting recovery-community and pet-owner interest overlap. Total: ~$300–450.
- **Fully scalable:** This is the **highest-scoring gap this cycle (32/40)** — worth building a small template family around it (sponsor/sponsee milestone variants: 30-day, 90-day, 1-year) using the same base art with swapped milestone text, scripted the same way as Brief 1/2's variant automation, cross-listed once validated.
- Confidence: well-grounded — both source niches (Recovery/sobriety 43 reviews, Pet breed+hobby 15) cleared the threshold. **Review Miner: used** — the wording-accuracy feature and shipping-reliability promise are drawn from real quoted complaints, not invented.

**BRIEF 5 — ADHD/Neurodivergent × Sobriety/Recovery** *(sensitive intersection — kept supportive/in-community, not sarcastic or mocking; grounded in genuine dual-diagnosis community language, not novelty)*
- **Exact title text:** "Neurodivergent Sober Sticker, Dual Diagnosis Recovery Decal, Subtle ADHD Sobriety Support Gift"
- **Listing description:** You've probably had someone explain either your brain or your recovery to you like it's simple. It's not, and you don't need a sticker that pretends otherwise. For adults holding both a neurodivergent diagnosis and a recovery journey — not a novelty item, a nod from someone who gets the overlap. Kiss-cut waterproof vinyl → holds up on a water bottle or laptop through daily use, not just the unboxing photo. Pastel neurodivergent color coding + sobriety's clean typography → reads as community-specific, not generic inspirational-quote merch. Correct, proofed wording → nothing to wince at when you hand it to someone. Add it to a recovery-milestone gift, or keep it for the days you need the reminder yourself.
- **On-product graphic text:** **"Neurodivergent AND Sober — Both Are Hard, Both Are Worth It"**
- **Style direction:** Pastel pride-flag color coding (from the ADHD/neurodivergent niche) combined with sobriety niche's clean inspirational-quote minimalism — no broken-chain/addiction iconography (avoid glamorizing/trivializing); soft rounded sans-serif type; simple sun-rising icon (shared growth/new-day motif).
- **Product type:** Sticker (kiss-cut vinyl), holographic finish optional.
- **Target audience:** Adults in dual-diagnosis recovery (neurodivergence + substance recovery) seeking community-affirming, non-mocking representation — likely reached via recovery support groups and ADHD community spaces, not general gift-shopping traffic.
- **Suggested price:** $3.50–$5 (convention, not comp-verified — recommend a small test batch given the smaller, more specific audience).
- **Under $100 to test:** Commission the sun-rising icon + typography (~$25–40), order a small proof batch (~$10–15), list on Etsy ($0.20). Total: ~$35–55.
- **Under $1,000 to scale:** This gap scored lowest on Profit Potential (4/10) of the five briefed — resist over-investing before the $100 test validates real demand. If it does, add 1–2 colorway variants and a very small Etsy Ads test (~$75–150) rather than the full budget used on higher-scoring gaps. Total: ~$110–190.
- **Fully scalable:** Only pursue past the $1,000 tier if the small test clearly outperforms this gap's below-average Profit Potential score — otherwise redirect scaling budget toward Gap 4 or Gap 2, which scored higher on both Market Opportunity and Profit Potential from the same review data.
- Confidence: well-grounded — both source niches (ADHD 52 reviews, Recovery/sobriety 43) cleared the threshold. **Review Miner: used** — the wording-accuracy feature is drawn from a real 3★ complaint in the sobriety sample, not invented.

**Brief 6 (Sober gardener / "recovery garden" stickers): still unbriefed.** Recovery/sobriety cleared the review threshold, but no Phrase Miner pass has run for that specific sub-angle — writing a brief now would mean inventing a phrase formula never actually reported, which Design Briefer's grounding rule prohibits. Candidate for a future cycle once Phrase Miner covers it, or once Gap 6's retire/keep call is resolved.

**BRIEF 7 — Pet Breed Identity × Romantasy/Cozy-Fantasy Reader Mug** *(more speculative than Briefs 3/4, which had real intersection near-misses; this brief synthesizes two separately-verified formulas with zero real combined-listing evidence, per Phrase Miner's own explicit "structural hypothesis, not a found pattern" flag)*
- **Exact title text:** "Custom [Breed] Dog Mom Mug, Fantasy Familiar Design, Romantasy Reader Gift for Dragon Rider Book Lovers" *(caveat: this exact title has zero real listing precedent — a mechanical combination of two verified-separately formulas, not something Phrase Miner or Trend Scout observed on a live listing)*
- **Listing description:** Your [Breed] isn't just a dog — she's the loyal, slightly feral companion who'd absolutely survive an enemies-to-lovers arc right alongside you, and she deserves to be on your mug, not just your camera roll. Made for romantasy readers who love their dog like a familiar and want their exact breed represented — not a generic "dog mom" mug that could belong to anyone. Custom breed name (purebred or portmanteau crossbreed) → your [Breed] gets the same specific recognition as any purebred, no generic "dog silhouette" filler. Trope-tag design (Dragon Rider / Fae / Morally Gray, your pick) → instantly signals your bookish identity to every fellow reader who spots it on your desk. Dishwasher- and microwave-safe ceramic → survives your actual morning routine, not just the photo. Personalize yours with your dog's breed before your next spicy fantasy read-through — because your familiar deserves main-character energy too. *(Hook is drafted copy, not a pulled buyer quote — Review Miner did not run this cycle.)*
- **On-product graphic text:** Two synthesized options, neither with real-listing precedent: **"MY FAMILIAR IS A [BREED NAME]"** (front, large), or **"DO NOT DISTURB: BOOK BOYFRIEND & DOG MOM ERA"** (side wrap, pulling Niche 2's verified "Do Not Disturb" framing).
- **Style direction:** **Synthesized hypothesis, not an observed pattern.** No listing in the actual pet-breed × romantasy intersection was found in any Trend Scout pass, including a dedicated image-focused pass. Everything below is stitched together from two separate parent niches, each itself split into two incompatible registers on 1–3 examples — treat as two candidate directions to check against real comps (a manual visual scan of actual listings), not as comp-verified art direction. **Direction A — Soft/cozy hybrid:** watercolor-style portrait of the pet's face with a subtle synthesized fantasy-creature accent (small wings or soft glow — this fusion itself is not attested in any real listing); soft pastel palette (lavender, blush, warm gold, drawn from one real "lavender dragon... candlelight" example); florals and candlelight as secondary elements (from the same real example). **Direction B — Line-art/doodle hybrid:** simple line-doodle of the pet's face with small fae/dragon sparkle accents (drawn from one real "diamond sparkle" fae example); higher-contrast palette (from one real "black dragon/gold dragon over mountains" example, treat as a single data point); optional "peekaboo" partial-pet framing behind a castle motif (combining one real Niche 1 layout term with one real recurring Niche 2 motif — the specific combination is synthesized, not observed). Typography/iconography intentionally left unspecified in both directions — no real example in either niche's data supports a specific lettering style or icon set. Recommend treating a manual visual comp scan of real pet-breed-mug and romantasy-mug listings as a Tier-1 prerequisite before committing to either direction.
- **Product type:** Mug (11oz ceramic, standard POD default) — the only product type either source formula names.
- **Target audience:** Romantasy/cozy-fantasy readers (BookTok/Bookstagram-adjacent) who own a specific, nameable dog breed and identify publicly as "dog mom/dad" — narrower than either source niche alone, consistent with Gap Finder's own "niche-within-a-niche-within-a-niche" framing.
- **Suggested price:** $19–$24 (convention-based estimate, **not comp-verified** — Gap Finder logged 0 resolvable listings for this combined gap, so there is no real comp price to anchor to).
- **Under $100 to test:** One design commission spanning both formulas (~$35–50 on Fiverr), one physical Printify sample mug (~$9–13 + shipping), Etsy's $0.20 listing fee, phone-shot listing photos. Total comfortably under $100 — treat this as a genuine unknown test given the gap's lowest Profit Potential score of any tracked gap, not a formality.
- **Under $1,000 to scale — gated, do not proceed automatically:** Recommend staying at the $100 tier until the Tier 1 listing produces real sales or favorite/view signal that disproves the gap's low Market Opportunity/Profit Potential scores. If it does: 3–5 popular-breed variants (Golden Retriever, French Bulldog, Australian Shepherd, Corgi, Dachshund) crossed with 2–3 trope tags (Dragon Rider, Fae, Morally Gray), a modest $150–300 Etsy Ads test, spot-check samples across variants. Total if funded: ~$300–450.
- **Fully scalable:** Once Tier 1 disproves the low Profit Potential score, a CSV of breed names × portmanteau crossbreed names × trope tags scripted into Printify's bulk/API tooling, cross-listed to Redbubble/TeePublic once validated on Etsy. Caveat: given the "niche-within-a-niche-within-a-niche" framing, automate only after Tier 1/2 signal justifies the addressable audience actually being large enough to matter.
- Confidence: **thin — more speculative than Briefs 3/4.** Zero combined-listing evidence; title/description/on-product copy are labeled synthesis of two real, separately-reported formulas (not invention); Style Direction is filled in as two labeled candidate hypotheses pending a real visual comp scan, not a verified style. Validation-stage only. **Review Miner: not used** (no `ETSY_API_KEY` — no real buyer-review text exists for either source niche this cycle); description copy is drafted marketing language, not an attributed buyer quote.

**BRIEF 8 — Dog-Breed × Profession × Dog-Mom Mug** *(revised 2026-09-03 — supersedes the original fully-synthesized Nurse × Golden Retriever framing with a real-evidence-grounded French Bulldog × "Coach" lead SKU; "Nurse" is preserved as a documented swappable profession variant, not discarded)*
- **Exact title text:** "French Bulldog Coach Mug, Funny Frenchie Mom Gift Coffee Cup" *(directly instantiates a real listing: https://www.etsy.com/listing/4364398645/french-bulldog-coach-mug-funny-frenchie — front-loads breed + profession, restates the offer with a product-noun synonym for secondary keyword coverage. Structure: `[Full Breed Name] [Profession] [Product Noun], Funny [Breed Nickname] Mom Gift [Product-Category Synonym]`, so far only confirmed on one real listing — still labeled a weak, 1-example pattern.)*
- **Listing description:** Most "dog mom" mugs treat every dog owner the same — one generic paw print, one generic joke that could belong to any breed. If your mornings start as a Frenchie mom first and a coach second, a mug that only knows the first half of that isn't really made for you. Made for French Bulldog owners who introduce themselves as "coach" right after they introduce their Frenchie, and want a mug that gets both parts of that identity instead of a generic dog-mom design. Personalized profession line ("Just a Coach who Loves Her Frenchie") → swap in your actual role so the mug reads like it was made for you, not pulled off a generic dog-mom shelf. Breed-specific "Frenchie" callout instead of a generic "dog mom" label → speaks directly to French Bulldog owners specifically, the exact audience this formula was pulled from. Dishwasher- and microwave-safe ceramic → fits your actual daily routine at home or at work, no special care required. Grab the Coach version now, or message us for a profession swap — Teacher, Nurse, Vet Tech, and more — before gift-giving season makes Frenchie-mom mugs an even more crowded lane. *(Hook is drafted copy, not a pulled buyer quote — Review Miner reported insufficient data this cycle, no `ETSY_API_KEY`.)*
- **On-product graphic text:** **"Just a Coach who Loves Her Frenchie"** — real copy from the listing above, structure `Just a [Profession Identity Noun] who Loves Her [Breed Nickname]`. Flagged: this is very likely an instance of the broader generic "Just a girl who loves her [X]" template rather than something bespoke to this breed/profession pairing — priced and marketed as such, not as a unique creative asset.
- **Style direction:** **Synthesized hypothesis, not observed data** (unchanged limitation from the original brief — no real palette/typography/layout was reported for this niche). The only real signal is a weak tone cue: "Funny" in the title implies lighthearted, phrase-forward copy rather than somber/minimalist. Starting-point hypothesis to test: text-forward layout (identity line as the visual centerpiece, not a large illustration), a simple Frenchie silhouette/icon as a secondary element rather than a detailed portrait, casual script or bold sans-serif typography. Treat exactly as Brief 7's Style Direction was handled — a hypothesis to validate with a physical sample, not a design brief to execute blind.
- **Product type:** Mug — Gap Finder's recommended lead SKU (year-round, vs. ornament's Q4-only window).
- **Target audience:** French Bulldog owners who work as coaches (athletic, life, or otherwise) and lead with both their profession and their "Frenchie mom" identity. - **Under $1,000 to scale — still gated, kept conservative:** Profit Potential is still held at only 6/10 (no sales-volume or review data behind either real listing), so this stays diagnostic rather than aggressive. Build out 3–4 additional profession variants (Teacher, Nurse, Vet Tech, plus a general "dog mom" fallback) on the same fixed French Bulldog template via a scripted text swap (~$40–60 in incremental samples, following the real pouch listing's own bundling precedent) rather than commissioning entirely new designs per profession. Run a modest Etsy Ads test ($150–300) on the Coach listing alone first; hold off expanding ad spend to the other profession variants until it shows real signal. Total: ~$190–360.

Only after the Coach version's actual sell-through is confirmed should the same swap-the-profession template be extended across the other five breeds Gap Finder flagged as saturated-but-unclaimed in this exact triple-stack.

**BRIEF 9 — Equestrian Dark Academia Wall Art** *(strongest evidence base of Gap 9's phrase formula, 3/3 real examples; reframed from "horse girl" identity framing to the decor angle that's actually evidenced)*
- **Exact title text:** "Equestrian Dark Academia Print, Gothic Horse Wall Art, Moody Victorian Digital Download"
- **Listing description:** Most equestrian wall art still defaults to bright, sunlit stable photography or pastel farmhouse prints — if your space leans moody, Victorian, and a little gothic, the horse art aisle hasn't caught up yet. Made for dark academia and gothic-decor lovers who want equestrian imagery without the bright barn-photo aesthetic that dominates the category. Painterly, vintage-reproduction rendering → reads like a found Victorian oil painting, not a stock photo cropped into a frame. Deep, moody color treatment (forest green, black, aged brown) → slots into a dark academia or gothic gallery wall without fighting the palette. Instant digital download → print tonight at home or a local shop, no shipping wait. Download it tonight and have your gothic equestrian print framed by the weekend. *(Hook is drafted copy, not a pulled buyer quote — Review Miner did not run this cycle.)*
- **On-product graphic text:** None — unbranded fine-art wall décor, matching the 3 real evidenced examples (pure imagery + title/tag copy, no on-print text overlay reported). Any added text treatment would be an untested addition beyond what Phrase Miner reported.
- **Style direction:** Deep, moody, desaturated palette (forest green, black, aged brown/sepia); painterly or vintage-reproduction rendering (brushwork, aged-canvas feel); classical/Victorian equestrian scene-setting (horse-into-water, polo player, thoroughbred portraiture); fine-art print composition with generous margin for standard frame sizes; minimal to no text overlay. *(Text-inferred from titles/descriptions only — no actual images were reviewed this cycle, no `ETSY_API_KEY`.)*
- **Product type:** Wall art — digital download primary, mailed physical print as secondary variant.
- **Target audience:** Dark academia / gothic home-decor enthusiasts (skews 20s–30s, apartment/rental gallery-wall builders) drawn to the aesthetic tag, not necessarily equestrians or riders — decor-driven, not identity-driven, per Gap Finder's explicit reframe away from "horse girl" identity language.

- **Under $100 to test:** One design commission for a single dark-academia equestrian scene (~$30–50, Fiverr). No physical sample strictly required for a digital-only listing; add one Printify print sample (~$15–25) if testing the mailed variant too. Etsy listing fees for both variants (~$0.40). Total: well under $100.
- **Under $1,000 to scale:** Strongest evidence base of the two new gaps (3/3) and scores support real investment (Ease of Entry 7, User Fit 8). Commission 4–6 additional scene variants matching the evidenced subject range (horse-into-lake, polo player, thoroughbred portrait, one more gothic scene) at ~$40–60 each (~$200–300); run a $300–400 Etsy Ads test splitting budget across the evidenced keyword tags ("dark academia," "gothic wall art," "equestrian print"); a handful of physical print samples across 2–3 sizes to check print/color fidelity (~$100–150). Total: ~$650–850.

**BRIEF 10 — RSD Stickers** *(thin — single-comp evidence base; grounded in Phrase Miner's formula and Gap Finder's rubric only, no Review Miner data available)*
- **Exact title text:** "RSD Isn't Too Much Sticker - Rejection Sensitive Dysphoria ADHD Awareness - Vinyl Decal for Spoonies & Invisible Illness"
- **Listing description:** Ever had a comment roll around in your head for three days when everyone else moved on in three minutes? That's not you being dramatic — that's RSD. Made for people with ADHD (and the spoonies and invisible-illness folks who already speak this language) who are tired of "you're too sensitive" and want a small, honest way to say *this is a real thing, not a personality flaw.* Waterproof matte vinyl → survives your water bottle, your laptop, your planner, and the days you don't want to explain yourself out loud. Clinical term + acronym on the design → doubles as quiet self-advocacy — hand someone the sticker instead of the whole explanation. Small, low-key sticker format → says it without shouting it, for the days you want the identity marker but not the conversation. Grab one for the water bottle that already has your spoon and your ADHD stickers — RSD belongs on there too. *(Hook is drafted from the clinically-recognized definition of RSD, not a pulled buyer quote — Review Miner reported insufficient data this cycle, no `ETSY_API_KEY`.)*
- **On-product graphic text:** **"RSD: The Sting Is Real"** paired with a small line-art mascot (a soft, rounded creature curling inward — e.g. hedgehog/armadillo shape — reading as "flinching/withdrawing," not clinical or diagnostic imagery), with a secondary small-print line beneath it: **"ADHD · Spoonie · Rejection Sensitive Dysphoria."** Deliberately does not reuse the one real comp's specific pun (that's the competitor's own joke, not a reusable formula element) — instead generalizes Phrase Miner's observed formula shape (validation-forward phrase + mascot illustration + clinical-term/slang keyword stack).
- **Style direction:** Cute, rounded mascot-character illustration as the visual anchor with a small supporting text stack below — the one concrete visual signal Phrase Miner reported (the single physical product standing out in an otherwise digital-worksheet-dominated category leans on humor + a character, not clinical/typography-only design). Palette/typography are **not comp-grounded** — convention-based estimate only (soft/muted tones — dusty rose, sage, lavender — consistent with the tracker's broader spoonie/chronic-illness sticker aesthetic, rounded sans-serif type): verify against a few more comps before locking palette.
- **Product type:** Die-cut or kiss-cut vinyl sticker — this brief is scoped to the sticker gap specifically; RSD wall art already has 3 real listings serving it (see Gaps section), so a wall-art SKU is not part of this brief.
- **Target audience:** Adults with ADHD — particularly late-diagnosed adults and women, overrepresented in the online ADHD self-diagnosis/community-slang space — who already use "spoonie" and "invisible illness" language for themselves and are buying a low-cost identity/validation item (water bottle, laptop, planner), not a clinical or symptom-management product.
- **Suggested price:** $4.50–$5.50 — **convention-based estimate only, not comp-verified.** No price was reported for the one real competitor listing found.
- **Under $100 to test:** Fiverr illustration commission (mascot + text lockup): ~$40–60. One physical Printify sample + shipping: ~$8–12. A second physical sample specifically to check tone/readability in hand (worth the extra sample given the flagged sensitive-topic tone risk — a mockup alone won't show whether the mascot+phrase reads as validating vs. flippant): ~$8–12. Etsy listing fee: $0.20. Total: ~$60–85.
- **Under $1,000 to scale — signal-gathering, not a scale-up commitment:** No buyer-language data exists for this niche (Review Miner had nothing), so there's no signal on which framing (ADHD-forward, spoonie-forward, RSD-only, combined) actually converts — this tier exists to generate that signal cheaply. 3 text/slang-stack variants on the same base mascot: ~$60–90. Physical samples across variants: ~$30–40. Conservative Etsy Ads test: $150–300. A small parallel test on a second cheap product format (magnet or pin) using the same art: ~$50–100. Total: ~$300–530, deliberately well under the $1,000 ceiling. Given Gap Finder's Profit Potential score is only 6/10 (sticker price ceiling caps upside even at good margin), don't increase ad spend past this range until real sales data disproves the moderate-profit-potential concern.
- **Fully scalable:** Script variant generation off the one master mascot file — swap slang-stack text (ADHD-only / spoonie-only / combined / acronym-only) and color to produce a small templated listing family from one base illustration. Use Printify's bulk/API upload to push the validated variant set as multiple SKUs without manual re-uploading. If (and only if) the sticker signal is positive, template the same base art across the adjacent product type already validated elsewhere in this niche family (wall art) via the same automated variant swap, rather than commissioning new art. If the "clinical-term + acronym + community-slang stack + validating mascot phrase" formula proves out, it's a reusable template worth testing against other ADHD/chronic-illness slang terms beyond RSD specifically — a follow-on decision, not part of this brief.
- Confidence: **thin — recommend more research before production spend beyond Tier 1.** Rests on a single real competitor listing (Phrase Miner's own words: "weak pattern from 1 example") and Gap Finder's rubric, with no comp pricing and no visual-style verification beyond one inferred data point. **Review Miner: not used** (insufficient data — no `ETSY_API_KEY`) — no real buyer complaints, desired outcomes, or purchase-driving phrases were used or invented anywhere in this brief, including the description hook.

### Gap Scores — niche-validation rubric (restated in full; row 11 rescored this cycle, rows 1-10 unchanged from Cycles 1/7/8/14/18)

1-10 per category, scored from real data gathered this cycle (Etsy listing counts, saturation levels, Review Miner findings where available) — never inferred from vibes alone. User Fit is scored specifically for this operator: CS student, technical/automation-minded, limited budget and time. Total = sum out of 40. Full scoring guidance lives in `.claude/agents/gap-finder.md`.

| Rank | Gap | Market Opportunity | Ease of Entry | User Fit | Profit Potential | Total | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Knightcore/Castlecore × teacher/nurse shirts | 7 | 6 | 5 | 7 | 25 | Real 0-listing gap against a medium-size validated niche (501 listings), but Review Miner had insufficient data (11 reviews) and t-shirt crest linework needs more illustration skill than a sticker — moderate fit for a solo operator starting out. |
| 2 | Nonnacore × ADHD/spoonie self-care mugs | 8 | 6 | 7 | 7 | 28 | 0-listing gap sitting between two large, active niches (3,962 + 3,204 listings) with strong real review grounding (52 + 54 reviews). Mug personalization adds per-order load, but it's a genuinely automatable field-templating problem — good fit for an automation-minded operator. |
| 3 | Gothmas × pet-mom Christmas stickers | 6 | 9 | 9 | 5 | 29 | Near-empty gap (1 loose near-miss) with strong review grounding on both sides (52 + 15 reviews) including a real, actionable shipping-reliability differentiator. Sticker = easiest possible entry and fully templatable, but Christmas-only seasonality and small per-unit sticker margin cap the profit ceiling. |
| 4 | Sobriety × pet-mom stickers/shirts | 7 | 9 | 9 | 7 | 32 | **Highest-scoring gap.** Functionally open gap between two solid niches (598 + 152 listings), strong review grounding (43 + 15 reviews), sticker-first entry with a tee upsell, and — unlike Gap 3 — no seasonality cap, so volume can be sustained year-round (sponsor/sponsee gifting, sobriety-anniversary milestones happen every month). |
| 5 | ADHD × sobriety/recovery | 6 | 9 | 9 | 4 | 28 | Fully open gap (0 listings) with strong review grounding on both sides (52 + 43 reviews), easy sticker entry, but the design brief's own audience note ("smaller, more specific audience") caps realistic volume — lowest Profit Potential among the original five briefed gaps despite otherwise strong scores. |
| 6 (stretch) | Sober gardener / "recovery garden" stickers | 4 | 6 | 7 | 3 | 20 | Lowest total among the original entries, consistent with Gap Finder's own original "weakest entry" note — verification was never re-queried (still inferred) and no Phrase Miner formula exists for this sub-angle, so both the opportunity and the entry cost are unverified. **As of Cycle 20, this is once again the tracker's single lowest-scoring gap** — Gap 11 (below) moved above it on rescore. |
| 7 (briefed) | Pet breed identity × Romantasy/cozy-fantasy reader mugs | 6 | 8 | 8 | 5 | 27 | 0 resolvable listings across 4 search-only query variants, sitting between one real-listing-count niche (pet breed combo mugs, 152 listings tracked) and one trending but still search-only-verified niche (romantasy mug). Ease of Entry and User Fit score well — personalization (breed/name selection) is scriptable via Printify variants. Profit Potential is the lowest of the original tracked gaps: this is a niche-within-a-niche-within-a-niche — the second axis is breed identity × book-genre/fandom identity. Market Opportunity capped at 6, not higher, because neither side's true overlap size is API-confirmed. |
| 8 (briefed, rescored 2026-09-03) | Dog-breed × profession × parent-identity mug/ornament | 7 | 8 | 9 | 6 | 30 | One real seller confirmed operating live in the exact 3-axis space across two breeds/product types (de-risking the templated-variant approach specifically), while 5 of 6 breeds checked still show zero real triple-stack listings — Market Opportunity and User Fit moved up from the original 28/40 on the strength of this proof-of-concept, while Ease of Entry and Profit Potential were held flat since nothing changed about production mechanics or sales-volume evidence. |
| 9 (briefed, reframed) | Equestrian-imagery × dark-academia wall art/prints | 6 | 7 | 8 | 7 | 28 | Strongest evidence base of the two Cycle 11 gaps (3/3 real listings support both a title/copy formula and a visual-style formula) — real transacting demand confirmed on both source aesthetics separately, but Market Opportunity held at 6 rather than higher since 3 active listings means some real competition already exists, not a genuinely near-empty (0-1) gap. Ease of Entry and User Fit both score well for a technical/automation-minded operator — digital-download wall art has near-zero marginal cost and is highly batchable. Profit Potential highest among gaps 1-9 (7): digital downloads carry no per-unit Printify cost and no seasonality, though the true dual-aesthetic-audience is narrower than either parent niche alone. |
| 10 (briefed) | Rejection Sensitive Dysphoria (RSD) stickers | 9 | 7 | 8 | 6 | 30 | Near-zero (1) confirmed physical-sticker supply sitting between two large, validated parent niches (3,962 + 3,204 listings) — the strongest Market Opportunity score of any tracked gap. Ease of Entry docked one point for genuine sensitive-topic tone risk despite otherwise simple static-linework mechanics. User Fit strong — one base illustration templates/scales across color/text variants with no per-order labor. Profit Potential moderate: sticker price point caps the ceiling even at high margin, and "RSD" as a literal acronym is a narrower sub-slice of the validated adjacent audiences, not the full audience size itself. No seasonality. |
| 11 (stretch, rescored 2026-09-09, not briefed) | Trad wife × Nonnacore domestic-homemaker wall art | 4 | 6 | 6 | 5 | 21 | **Rescored from 18/40.** A confirmed real (if apparel-weighted) market page and wall-art listing growth (1→6-7 real listings) is a mild positive demand-adjacency signal (Market Opportunity +1), and doubles as a mechanical proof-of-concept that the templated-variant production model works for this niche (Ease of Entry +1). Both are offset by the same evidence confirming more existing supply than previously tracked, and by a likely single-shop-concentration pattern meaning the raw listing count may overstate independent competition (Market Opportunity held conservative despite the bump). User Fit unchanged — the new evidence confirms rather than adds to the "templatable once a base illustration exists" assumption the original score already rested on. Profit Potential nudged up slightly (+1) on the same mild positive market-page signal, still capped by unverified addressable-audience size and zero price/sales data on any of the new listings. |

**What this makes explicit:** Gap 11's rescore (18→21/40) moves it back above Gap 6 (20/40) — **Gap 6 is again the tracker's single lowest-scoring tracked gap**, correcting Cycle 19's now-stale claim to the contrary. Gap 4 remains the highest-scoring gap overall (32/40); Gaps 8 and 10 remain tied for second (30/40). No gap's briefing status changed this cycle — Gap 11 stays unbriefed per Gap Finder's own conclusion that the new evidence, while directionally positive, doesn't clear the bar.

### Design Briefs (restated in full — unchanged this cycle; no new brief was written since Gap 11 remains below the briefing bar. Briefs 1-5/7/8/9/10 unchanged from Cycles 1/8/9/11/14/19, Brief 6 still unbriefed)

Titles: primary keyword front-loaded in the first 3 words, lead with outcome not feature, "best/amazing/premium/quality" banned outright. Descriptions: hook → "who this is for" line → `[Feature] → [benefit]` bullets → specific CTA. See `.claude/agents/design-briefer.md` for the full rule set.

**BRIEF 1 — Knightcore × School Nurse/Teacher**
- **Exact title text:** "School Nurse Knight Tee, Healer of the Realm Medieval Shirt, Comfort Colors Ren Faire Nurse Gift, Fantasy Educator Appreciation"
- **Listing description:** Tired of nurse-week gifts that could've come from anyone? This one couldn't. Made for school nurses and clinical educators who'd rather wear a crest than a cartoon cupcake mug — the ones who read romantasy on lunch break and know exactly what "Healer of the Realm" means. Comfort Colors garment-dyed cotton → broken-in soft the day it arrives, not after ten washes. Hand-drawn medieval crest linework → reads as real art up close, not clipart across a classroom. Unisex fit → grab your size off the standard chart, no guessing. Order before nurse appreciation week to guarantee it's on her desk, not in transit.
- **On-product graphic text:** **"HEALER OF THE REALM"** with small subtext **"School Nurse Est. [Year]"**
- **Style direction:** Comfort Colors garment-dyed faded tee (sage or faded black); minimalist medieval line-art icon — a caduceus redrawn as a crossed sword-and-staff crest; goth-floral border (roses/thorns) around the crest; centered badge/crest layout, single ink color for the linework.
- **Product type:** T-shirt
- **Target audience:** School nurses and clinical educators who read fantasy romance/romantasy or are into cottagecore-adjacent medieval aesthetics — gift-buyable for nurse appreciation week/graduation.
- **Suggested price:** $26–$28 (comp-verified: real castlecore/knightcore Comfort Colors tees on Etsy run $26.05–$28; one outlier listing at $68.89, treat as non-representative).
- **Under $100 to test:** Commission a single crest illustration via Fiverr (~$25–40), upload to Printify (free), order one physical sample tee (~$15–20 incl. shipping), list on Etsy ($0.20 fee). Total: ~$45–65.
- **Under $1,000 to scale:** Commission 3–4 colorway/subtext variants of the crest (~$100–150), list across 2–3 blank colors (sage, faded black, faded navy), run a small Etsy Ads test (~$150–300 over 1–2 months), sample each variant (~$60–80). Total: ~$400–700.
- **Fully scalable:** Template the same crest-and-motto formula across other occupation×subculture combos (medic, vet-tech, dental-hygienist) — swap subtext programmatically via Printify's API, batch-upload via their bulk tooling, and feed real sales data back into this tracker next cycle to replace the assumed conversion rate with actuals.
- Confidence: partially grounded — style/phrase formulas solid, but Castlecore/Knightcore's review sample this cycle was too thin (11 reviews, need 15+) for real buyer-language grounding. **Review Miner: not used** (insufficient data) — description hook is written from general market positioning, not quoted buyers.

**BRIEF 2 — Nonnacore × ADHD/Spoonie Self-Care Mug**
- **Exact title text:** "Spoonie Self-Care Mug, Nonna Says Rest Now, Personalized Chronic Illness Gift, Cozy Permission To Pause"
- **Listing description:** Some mornings the only productive thing you do is convince yourself it's okay to sit back down. This mug says that out loud, gently, before you even ask. Made for spoonies who want their self-care merch to feel like a hug from Nonna, not another wellness lecture. Custom name on the rim → unmistakably yours, or exactly right as a gift for the spoonie in your life. Dishwasher-safe ceramic → survives real mornings, not just the photo shoot. Cozy majolica-floral border → doesn't announce "sick" to anyone who glances at your desk, it just looks like your favorite mug. Add the name that needs the reminder most and let Nonna do the talking.
- **On-product graphic text:** **"Nonna Said Sit Down And Rest"** with small script tagline **"[Custom Name]'s Spoonie Corner"**

(cozy permission-giving tone, not edgy/sarcastic); small holographic spoon icon tucked into the floral border as a nod to spoon theory.

A second physical sample specifically to check tone/readability in hand (worth the extra sample given the flagged sensitive-topic tone risk — a mockup alone won't show whether the mascot+phrase reads as validating vs. flippant): ~$8–12. Etsy listing fee: $0.20. Total: ~$60–85.
- **Under $1,000 to scale — signal-gathering, not a scale-up commitment:** No buyer-language data exists for this niche (Review Miner had nothing), so there's no signal on which framing (ADHD-forward, spoonie-forward, RSD-only, combined) actually converts — this tier exists to generate that signal cheaply. 3 text/slang-stack variants on the same base mascot: ~$60–90. Physical samples across variants: ~$30–40. Conservative Etsy Ads test: $150–300. A small parallel test on a second cheap product format (magnet or pin) using the same art: ~$50–100. Total: ~$300–530, deliberately well under the $1,000 ceiling. Given Gap Finder's Profit Potential score is only 6/10 (sticker price ceiling caps upside even at good margin), don't increase ad spend past this range until real sales data disproves the moderate-profit-potential concern.
- **Fully scalable:** Script variant generation off the one master mascot file — swap slang-stack text (ADHD-only / spoonie-only / combined / acronym-only) and color to produce a small templated listing family from one base illustration. Use Printify's bulk/API upload to push the validated variant set as multiple SKUs without manual re-uploading. If (and only if) the sticker signal is positive, template the same base art across the adjacent product type already validated elsewhere in this niche family (wall art) via the same automated variant swap, rather than commissioning new art. If the "clinical-term + acronym + community-slang stack + validating mascot phrase" formula proves out, it's a reusable template worth testing against other ADHD/chronic-illness slang terms beyond RSD specifically — a follow-on decision, not part of this brief.
- Confidence: **thin — recommend more research before production spend beyond Tier 1.** Rests on a single real competitor listing (Phrase Miner's own words: "weak pattern from 1 example") and Gap Finder's rubric, with no comp pricing and no visual-style verification beyond one inferred data point. **Review Miner: not used** (insufficient data — no `ETSY_API_KEY`) — no real buyer complaints, desired outcomes, or purchase-driving phrases were used or invented anywhere in this brief, including the description hook.

**Pricing scope note:** All sticker prices (Briefs 3, 4, 5, 10), Brief 7's mug price, and Briefs 8/9's prices are convention-based estimates, not live comps — verify against `site:etsy.com` pricing in these specific sub-niches before finalizing, once `ETSY_API_KEY` or a page-fetch tool is available.

**Run log:** completed 2026-09-11 · next cycle scheduled 2026-09-12 · Trend Scout: success (two-term targeted re-check of Gaps 3 and 4, search fallback — no `ETSY_API_KEY`, 22nd consecutive cycle unset; direct `curl` to etsy.com also blocked by the outbound proxy, 403) · Phrase Miner: skipped (neither re-check surfaced a listing in either gap's actual target product category — sticker/shirt — to extract a title/copy formula from; the bandana and the coin are both off-category finds) · Gap Finder: success (Gap 4 rescored on new demand-adjacency evidence; Gap 3 reviewed against new evidence, no change) · Review Miner: skipped (no `ETSY_API_KEY`; no gap newly cleared the briefing bar) · Design Briefer: skipped (Gap 4 already has Brief 4 and the rescore didn't change any design direction — only a score-citation number, which I updated directly rather than re-running Design Briefer for)

**Scope decision: partial recheck, two sub-agents.** No CEO Summary existed yet for today when this cycle started. Following this routine's standing guidance, a new calendar day is reason enough to consider a narrow re-check without requiring a full pipeline sweep. Cycle 21's own "Open follow-ups" list carried an explicit, unblocked item: "Re-query the two 1-listing near-miss gaps (Gothmas × Pet-Mom Christmas, Sobriety × Pet-Mom) periodically — still deferred pending API key restoration." Because one of the two findings had real scoring implications, I continued to Gap Finder for a targeted re-score of just those two gaps. Phrase Miner, Review Miner, and Design Briefer weren't needed: neither re-check surfaced a listing in the actual target product category, no `ETSY_API_KEY` is available for Review Miner, and Gap 4's score bump didn't change any design-direction element of the already-written Brief 4 — only a number the brief cites, which I updated myself as a factual-consistency correction rather than commissioning a fresh Design Briefer pass over an unchanged design.

**Trend Scout's findings, reviewed.** No `ETSY_API_KEY` this session (still unset, 22nd consecutive cycle). Direct `curl` to etsy.com was attempted again and blocked by the outbound proxy (`connect_rejected — organization policy`, HTTP 403) — same failure mode as Cycle 21, noted honestly rather than silently worked around. All findings are WebSearch-index-based. **Gap 3 (Gothmas × pet-mom Christmas stickers):** still no genuine match across 8 independently-phrased queries. The closest new find — a "GOTHMAS BANDANA... Pentagram Candy Cane Bandana" — is a real gothic-Christmas pet product, but it's worn by the dog, not a sticker framed around human dog-mom identity, so it's wrong on both product type and framing. No dedicated combined market page exists. Essentially unchanged from Cycle 1's read. **Gap 4 (Sobriety × pet-mom stickers/shirts):** a real, resolvable listing — "Sober Dog Mom or Dog Dad Coin, Sobriety Gift, Customizable, Personalized, Bronze, Dog Dad, Dog Mom" — recurred across 5 of 7 query variants and genuinely fuses sobriety/recovery framing with dog-mom/dog-dad identity, a materially stronger signal than Cycle 1's near-miss (which had zero sobriety framing at all). It's a coin/keychain, though, not a sticker or shirt — the gap's target product category remains unserved (0 of 7 queries found a sticker/shirt match). No dedicated combined market page exists for either gap.

**Gap Finder's re-score, reviewed.** Gap Finder independently spot-checked both listing URLs before scoring (both resolved). **Gap 3: no change (stays 29/40).** The bandana is weak, off-target signal — it shows a seller is willing to combine "Gothmas" with "dog," but says nothing about the human-identity sticker angle the gap is actually about, and it's not competitive supply in the target category. Ease of Entry, User Fit, and Profit Potential held flat: the coin is a *more* operationally-loaded, personalization-heavy product than a sticker, so it isn't evidence that sticker/shirt entry mechanics, this operator's automation fit, or price/volume data changed at all — if anything it's a soft argument that the sticker-first path stays the easier one, not something that moves an already-high 9.

Still the tracker's highest-scoring gap.** Functionally open gap between two solid niches (598 + 152 listings), strong review grounding (43 + 15 reviews), sticker-first entry with a tee upsell, and — unlike Gap 3 — no seasonality cap, so volume can be sustained year-round (sponsor/sponsee gifting, sobriety-anniversary milestones happen every month). **Market Opportunity 7→8:** a real, recurring (5/7 queries) listing directly fusing sobriety and dog-mom/dog-dad identity — a coin/keychain, not a sticker/shirt — sharpens confidence that real buyer demand exists at this exact concept intersection, while confirming the sticker/shirt product category itself remains unserved (0 of 7 queries). Ease of Entry, User Fit, and Profit Potential held flat — no new production, operator-fit, or price/volume data to justify moving them; the coin is, if anything, a *harder* production model (personalization-heavy) than the sticker-first path already scored here. |
| 5 | ADHD × sobriety/recovery | 6 | 9 | 9 | 4 | 28 | Fully open gap (0 listings) with strong review grounding on both sides (52 + 43 reviews), easy sticker entry, but the design brief's own audience note ("smaller, more specific audience") caps realistic volume — lowest Profit Potential among the original five briefed gaps despite otherwise strong scores. |

Ease of Entry, User Fit, and Profit Potential unchanged — this evidence is about market emptiness, not execution difficulty, design fit, or price/volume data. **As of this cycle, this is the tracker's single lowest-scoring gap** (Gap 4's rescore this cycle didn't change that — Gap 11 at 23/40 already sat above it). Not briefing-ready: no Phrase Miner formula pass has ever run for this sub-angle. |
| 7 (briefed) | Pet breed identity × Romantasy/cozy-fantasy reader mugs | 6 | 8 | 8 | 5 | 27 | 0 resolvable listings across 4 search-only query variants, sitting between one real-listing-count niche (pet breed combo mugs, 152 listings tracked) and one trending but still search-only-verified niche (romantasy mug). Ease of Entry and User Fit score well — personalization (breed/name selection) is scriptable via Printify variants. Profit Potential is the lowest of the original tracked gaps: this is a niche-within-a-niche-within-a-niche — the second axis is breed identity × book-genre/fandom identity. Market Opportunity capped at 6, not higher, because neither side's true overlap size is API-confirmed. |
| 8 (briefed, rescored 2026-09-03) | Dog-breed × profession × parent-identity mug/ornament | 7 | 8 | 9 | 6 | 30 | One real seller confirmed operating live in the exact 3-axis space across two breeds/product types (de-risking the templated-variant approach specifically), while 5 of 6 breeds checked still show zero real triple-stack listings — Market Opportunity and User Fit moved up from the original 28/40 on the strength of this proof-of-concept, while Ease of Entry and Profit Potential were held flat since nothing changed about production mechanics or sales-volume evidence. |
| 9 (briefed, reframed) | Equestrian-imagery × dark-academia wall art/prints | 6 | 7 | 8 | 7 | 28 | Strongest evidence base of the two Cycle 11 gaps (3/3 real listings support both a title/copy formula and a visual-style formula) — real transacting demand confirmed on both source aesthetics separately, but Market Opportunity held at 6 rather than higher since 3 active listings means some real competition already exists, not a genuinely near-empty (0-1) gap. Ease of Entry and User Fit both score well for a technical/automation-minded operator — digital-download wall art has near-zero marginal cost and is highly batchable. Profit Potential highest among gaps 1-9 (7): digital downloads carry no per-unit Printify cost and no seasonality, though the true dual-aesthetic-audience is narrower than either parent niche alone. |

Ease of Entry (+1) and User Fit (+1) moved on the same new evidence: verbatim, repeated production specs (AI-generated art, standard archival paper spec, defined size range, tiered discount) across 9 listings is a genuine, replicable mechanical proof-of-concept for a cheap production pathway, and maps closely onto this operator's automation-minded, low-budget profile — more concrete than Cycle 20's inference-only read.
